# Homeground Topic Universe 1000

Snapshot: 2026-08-11
Repository baseline: `origin/main@0d66aebaee9f0a0253833a55c37a3ca584484853`
Validated canonical identities: **1000**

This is a canonical search-task map, not a writing queue. English, Simplified Chinese and Korean locale versions count as one identity. No mapped topic authorizes an Issue, article, public URL, indexation or publication.

## Identity state

| State | Count |
| --- | --- |
| mapped-validated | 958 |
| published | 38 |
| completed-not-published | 3 |
| in-production | 1 |

## Evidence limits

- Live Search Console API: **unavailable**. A local export is available, but totals conflict between chart and page tables; it is used directionally only.
- Keyword volume, CPC, keyword difficulty and purchase probability: **unavailable**.
- Google autocomplete and sampled SERPs: qualitative query/task evidence only; autocomplete is not volume.
- Sampled PAA block: **unavailable** for the observed query. Google Trends is a relative index, not volume.

## Priority model

`priorityScore` is a relative editorial score, not search volume or a traffic forecast. It synthesizes observed demand class, task clarity, decision proximity, coverage gap, Homeground information gain, source and asset feasibility, internal-link value, business role, freshness cost and duplicate risk; `priorityReason` records the explanation. Published owners receive 100 because protecting canonical ownership outranks replacing them. `priorityRank` provides one global 1–1,000 order with slug-based deterministic tie-breaking.

## Why the map is divided this way

The 9 approved sections follow a traveller journey rather than a keyword list: discovery and understanding lead into route/time/stay decisions; transport and essentials help execution and recovery; tools compress repeat decisions only when structured data is governable; services appear only where a traveller is choosing a real level of support. The 27 collections keep one canonical task owner while allowing natural parent, child and sibling links.

| Section | Identities | Primary entry role | Natural next link |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| explore | 137 | Place and attraction discovery | season, route, stay area |
| plan | 122 | City order, pace and trade-offs | transport, booking, support level |
| transport | 117 | Airport, rail and transfer execution | stay geography, route planning, recovery |
| when-to-go | 112 | Season, holiday and event fit | destination and booking windows |
| stay | 115 | Area, property type and foreign-guest access | transport and itinerary fit |
| essentials | 98 | Entry, payment, connectivity and recovery | in-trip guides and consultation when stuck |
| culture | 146 | History, food, arts and social understanding | places, experiences and practical etiquette |
| tools | 71 | Repeat decisions using governed data | the explanatory canonical guide or service |
| services | 82 | Choosing guide, transfer, booking or whole-trip support | honest consultation/scope page |

## Traffic, authority and business connection

- Discovery entrances come mainly from cities, regions, attractions, history, food and arts. They build entity authority and route readers toward a concrete visit decision instead of forcing a sales CTA.
- Planning entrances connect city order, season, stay area and transport. These pages should pass a traveller to the next unresolved task, not repeat the same itinerary in several URLs.
- In-trip utility entrances cover hubs, payments, connectivity, booking and recovery. They need the strictest source-review modules because errors can disrupt a real trip.
- Culture forms authority through source-rich entity clusters: a history/idea owner can link to a place, a visit task, a food practice and a real experience without turning culture into an advertisement.
- Commercial-intent and conversion-support identities are deliberately bounded. They explain whether self-service, a local guide, a transfer, booking help or whole-trip support changes the outcome; they do not manufacture service availability.
- Tool candidates are specifications until a data contract, QA, accessible fallback, maintenance owner and central approval exist. A calculator shell is not counted merely because it can be coded.

## Highest maintenance zones

Entry policy, airport/rail operations, holiday calendars, events, ticketing, payments/connectivity, prices and tool data have the highest freshness burden. They require module-level source review dates and update owners. Stable cultural and historical pages still require primary or academic sourcing but usually need event-driven rather than frequent operational updates.

## Why this is not 1,000 template articles

Each identity carries a specific search task, traveller outcome, canonical boundary, inclusions/exclusions, evidence class, source plan, asset plan and maintenance rule. Locale variants count once. City/month/nationality/day-count/route-direction matrices, synonym pages, FAQ fragments and ungoverned annual URLs are excluded in the conflict ledger. 119 raw seeds have explicit merge/reject decisions; none is discarded only to make a quota fit.

## Governance

Employee 7 maps and proposes → the central editor approves, merges into an existing owner, requests evidence or rejects → only after approval is a GitHub Issue created → employees 1–6 draft → the deputy integrator reviews assets and locales → the central editor merges and publishes → employee 7 records publication, performance and duplicate protection.

All new indexable SEO pages should pass through the Search Map in principle. Business owners may propose topics, but canonical ownership and page shape must still be checked. Central editors may start urgent updates; employee 7 records them afterward. Employee 7 has proposal authority only.
